The batch path has been seen working (DL3) and failing (DL5.0), exactly like the per-slot path — the **platform** is the differentiator, not the verb. Replacing it was still right, for addressability; the stated reason was wrong and would have become lore.

**Second review pass, over the fixes themselves.** Four more, all fixed: (a) `sActivationInFlight` could **latch for the process** — it is released only from the activation callback, and `ClusterManager` posts no deadline of its own on the warm/DL5 paths, so a `sendInfo` callback that never arrives (half-open ADB-TCP, the documented D50F_LC condition) delivered neither `onDisplayReady` nor `onDisplayTimeout` and left the Activate button silently dead; the guard now expires after 60 s (a real sequence is ~6.5 s, the DL4 probe ≤13 s) and is reclaimed. (b) `doActivatePreset`'s catch delivered a bare `e.getMessage()`, which is **null** for `DeadObjectException` — precisely what a killed daemon throws from `binder.transact` — and a null error is the wire value both consumers read as *"ran to completion"*: the failed preset would have been written to `LayoutPrefs` as the favourite and toasted as "partially activated"; failures now always carry the exception class name (also English, also greppable). (c) `ERR_BUSY` produced **no toast at all**, so a rejected second tap left the button looking dead during a diagnostic campaign — it now re-states "Activating …", which is the truth. (d) the Layouts-mode gate wrote nothing to the journal, making such a capture indistinguishable from "the tester never tried". Also: the daemon stamps `build=` on **every** `ATTACH_SLOT` block, not only in `main()` (the startup line is the log's first line and the report ships the last 400, so on a long-lived daemon it is always out of frame — the exact reused-stale-daemon case it was added for); `stageRef` starts at `queued-never-dispatched` so a 2 s timeout no longer names a stage that never ran; `step3a` also prints `displayCtx`'s identity, since `wm.addView` runs through *that* context's WindowManager; `markAutoStartFailed` now counts `mFreeZoneKeys` when deciding to `stopAll()`, so a free-zone-only layout cannot be orphaned on the cluster; and the `sAutoStartFired` re-arm moved **inside** `synchronized (FissionOrchestrator.class)` — it is a read-modify-write on the main thread against a static that `markAutoStartFailed` writes from the fission-exec thread.

**Known limitation, stated rather than papered over.** The manual Activate runs cluster projection *before* contacting the daemon, so a platform that fails at that first stage produces no `[ATTACH_SLOT]` trace at all. That gate is correct product behaviour — creating overlays on a display nobody scans out is defect (1) above — and on DL4 the decisive evidence arrives by another route regardless: `ClusterManager.logDisplayResolutionOnce` emits `display resolution — shellOk=… app=[…] daemon=[…]` during activation, which is the DisplayManagerService-whitelist verdict and does not depend on Layout. On DL5 activation is known to work (1.6.105 on-car), so a capture should reach the traced region. Not addressed here, and deliberately not: a re-entrancy guard covering the ~20 s auto-start window would have meant late surgery on the auto-start path every DiLink 3 tester depends on.

Three independent adversarial review lenses plus a fourth pre-release pass over the fixes themselves. One new string in all 13 locales (`lm_layout_mode_disabled`). No daemon protocol change; standard projection path byte-identical; DL3/DL4/DL5 unaffected. Build + lint 0/0, 177 unit tests green.

### 1.8.5-beta (versionCode 595)

**Names the two uid-2000 daemons apart, and stops one's binder being paired with the other's DESCRIPTOR.** Structural clarification plus one leak fix; **no user-visible change**.

**Why.** DashCast drives the cluster through TWO uid-2000 processes and the code never said which does what: the **ProxyDaemon "DOES"** things (shell + one-shot verbs — activation, launch, resize, force-stop; if it dies you retry the command), the **SurfaceDaemon "HOLDS"** things (the preview mirror, and on the cluster the Layout slot overlay windows with their TRUSTED VirtualDisplays, plus touch injection; if it dies the graphical state is lost). The name `MirrorDaemon` hid the second half — it owns **cluster** surfaces, not just the preview — and that misunderstanding had already produced the same defect **twice**: `ClusterMirrorManager.stopPreview` and `ClusterResizeActivity` both wrote `SurfaceDaemon.DESCRIPTOR` onto `ProxyClient.getDaemonBinder()`, the **proxy** daemon's binder, where `enforceInterface` rejects it and the transact silently does nothing. Consequences: on `stopProjectionNoAdb` / `onDestroy` the surface daemon was never told to drop its SurfaceControl token, so it kept compositing after a stop; and the resize editor's live preview has never worked.

**What changed.** The class is renamed `MirrorDaemon` → `SurfaceDaemon` — **CODE identity only**. Every wire/persisted identity is byte-identical (ServiceManager name `byd_mirror_daemon`, the DESCRIPTOR, transaction codes, both process names, `/data/local/tmp` paths, broadcast action/extra/permission, the `--- MIRRORDAEMON LOG ---` report section, the `MirrorDaemon` log TAG), because a daemon spawned by an older APK keeps running across an update and because triage greps historical reports; verified against the release APK's dex. **More important than the class name**: three accessors called `getDaemonBinder()` returned two different daemons — that is the identifier through which the mistake was actually made. They are now `getProxyDaemonBinder()` / `getSurfaceDaemonBinder()`, so a missed call site is a compile error. `FissionClient`'s duplicate `ServiceManager` lookup now delegates to the single resolver, and the boundary docs were corrected to be **true**: the first wording asserted a "single accessor" invariant that did not hold, and an auditor trusting it would have missed the entire Layout subsystem, the main preview and every touch.

**Noise removed.** The in-app mirror and in-app injection fallbacks (**235 failures / 0 successes** across the report corpus — an unprivileged app cannot hold `ACCESS_SURFACE_FLINGER`, which is why the daemon exists) now run **once per process** then skip, at WARN instead of ERROR; the old ERROR named a permission and read like a security regression, misleading several triages. They are **not deleted**: there is no formal proof the permission is denied, only 235/0, and deleting would break any unit where it does work.

**Deliberately reverted.** Pointing the resize editor at the correct binder was implemented, reviewed and taken back out: the surface daemon owns exactly **one** mirror, so a working call there re-targets it away from MainActivity's preview and nothing restores it on close (`MirrorCoordinator.attemptStart` short-circuits on a stale `mMirrorActive`), leaving the main preview frozen. Trading a preview nobody has ever had for a broken one is a bad deal — the editor keeps today's behaviour, the wrong pairing is gone from the tree, and the correct implementation is documented.

Two adversarial review rounds (the second caught that regression). No daemon protocol change; DL3/DL4/DL5 identical. Build + lint 0/0, 177 unit tests green.

### 1.8.4-beta (versionCode 594)

**DiLink 4.0: reach the cluster through the uid-2000 daemon, because the firmware hides it from the app process** (`INC-20260727-203241`, plus four earlier DL4 reports).

**Root cause, proven.** On DL4 the cluster VirtualDisplay EXISTS and sits idle and ready — `Display 1: fission_bg_xdjaVirtualSurface, 1920x720, layerStack 1, owner com.xdja.containerservice, state ON` in every DL4 capture, and ActivityTaskManager lists it — but the OEM patched `DisplayManagerService.getDisplayIdsInternal` with an app whitelist that refuses our uid the display-id list. From `INC-20260713-180803`, the one DL4 capture whose logcat still covered an activation: 378× `forbid for:10167`, 252× `getDisplayIdsInternal isPermittedApp:false` (never once true), 126× `reject for:10167 to get id` — uid 10167 **is** DashCast. That gate does not exist in AOSP and does not appear on DL3. So DashCast polled `DisplayManager` ~68 times over 80 s, logged **zero** candidates and timed out, while a uid-2000 shell dump 29 s later listed the display plainly.

Two corollaries the evidence forces: the DL4 display **pre-exists** the activation sequence (the set DMS iterated never changed across 54.6 s, before and after cmd 16/35), so the comment claiming cmd 35 creates it ~8-9 s later was a mis-inference and the earlier 12→20 s timeout bump rested on it; and **DL4 has never worked** — five reports, three app generations including pre-Kotlin `1.6.109`, with the ADAS-window-fix toggle already tested five times. Raising the timeout was never going to help: the display is not late, it is hidden.

**The fix.** The daemon sees what the app cannot, and already launches apps and drives the mirror, so on **DL4 only** the cluster is discovered by parsing `dumpsys display` through the uid-2000 shell, and the pipeline was reworked to run from injected values (id/width/height/layerStack) since the app can never hold a real `Display` there. Every touched site is gated on `Platform.isDiLink4()`; DL3/DL5 are provably untouched (`isDiLink3` is *defined* as `!DL2 && !DL4 && !DL5`, and the new registry's single writer sits behind the DL4 gate) — verified site by site and by replaying the new parser against all 107 real captures. The enumerator **refuses displays it may not drive** (`FLAG_PRIVATE`, third-party uid): a privileged `dumpsys` lists *every* logical display unlike the app API, so a foreign `dudu-auto-ui-pip` display — present in four tester reports — would otherwise have been driven, mirrored and launched onto. When nothing drivable is found the user is told projection is unavailable on this firmware rather than the generic "disconnected".

**Diagnosis.** This incident was nearly unsolvable because the report's own logs hid the proof: the tag-filtered logcat pinned `DisplayManagerService` at `:I`, dropping exactly the DEBUG-level whitelist refusals — now `:V`, with `ClusterManager:V` alongside. A truncated report now says so out loud instead of looking like a device that produced nothing. The activation log no longer claims to send `30→16→35` while sending `16→35`. The in-app SurfaceControl mirror is no longer attempted with no display, which was emitting ERROR-level `ACCESS_SURFACE_FLINGER?` noise that reads like a security regression. The post-OTA daemon bootstrap stops printing two contradictory verdicts.

**Honest limit.** It remains **unproven** whether the firmware also blocks cross-display activity launches for our uid — the same whitelist could apply there. This ships to *learn* as much as to fix; the new `display resolution — app=[…] daemon=[…] shellOk=…` line is designed to settle it from the next capture, and in particular to distinguish "the firmware hides it from uid 2000 too" from "the shell never answered", which were indistinguishable until now. No daemon protocol change; 2 strings × 13 locales; 176 unit tests, lint 0/0. Two rounds of adversarial review (first: 1 HIGH + 5 MEDIUM; second: confirmed the fixes, caught one dead self-heal and a report-size change doing more harm than good — the logcat work was cut back to the part that carries the value).

### 1.8.2-beta (versionCode 592)

The cluster fills the panel again: a projected app was losing **40% of the screen** to black borders by default, with no setting having been changed (`INC-20260725-211405`).

**The defect.** A tester reported "informazioni mancanti" — the app on the cluster was cut off on all four sides. The report's own cluster capture measured it exactly: content at **1600×522 inside a 1920×720 panel**, offset by 160/100 — precisely **double** the configured 80/50 margin. Double, because the margin was applied twice by two mechanisms that were never meant to stack: `applyClusterFreeformBounds` set the task's **launch bounds** to `Rect(80,50,1840,670)`, shrinking the task to 1760×620 before the app drew anything, and a display-level **`wm overscan 80,50,80,50`** then shrank the content *again* inside that already-smaller task. It was therefore never an over-aggressive setting but double counting — and 80/50 were the **built-in defaults**, so every unit shipped this way.

**The fix.** The global overscan feature is removed rather than re-tuned: halving the default would have hidden the bug, and per-app resizing with a saved size already covers the real need (including a bezel that genuinely crops — you compensate on the apps you actually project). Gone: the 80/50 default, the entire Settings card (sliders, ± buttons, visual mode, safe-zone mockup), the automatic application on cluster connect, and the symmetric per-app inset sliders that fed the same doubled path and defaulted to the same global value. Kept: the hand-drawn rectangle, now the single mechanism that shrinks anything — no rectangle means the app fills the panel. The cluster panel's **Adjust** button opens that editor directly so the one remaining mechanism keeps a visible entry point.

**Two cleanups, because neither state clears itself.** (a) `DashCastApp` wipes the stored global margins, per-app insets and saved rectangles **once** on first launch: values tuned against the doubled geometry describe a panel that no longer exists, and the global would otherwise stay applied with no UI left to reach it. (b) `ClusterService` issues `wm overscan reset` on **every** cluster connect, because that state lives in WindowManager rather than in our preferences — a unit updating from a build that set 80,50 would keep the inset forever. Both target the **resolved** cluster display id, never a hardcoded `1` (a fission VirtualDisplay has been observed as display 3) and never display 0; the stop-projection path, which was hardcoded to `-d 1`, is fixed to match — on a unit where the cluster is not display 1 it was resetting the wrong display and leaving the cluster's own inset in place.

Net **−1074 lines** over 29 files, including 195 unused strings across thirteen locales plus the orphaned styles, drawables and layout. No daemon protocol change; projection, mirroring, touch, Layout, CAN/HUD and OTA untouched. DiLink 5 was never affected (`wm overscan` was removed at API 30). 162 unit tests green, lint 0/0.

### 1.6.148-beta (versionCode 589)

Fixes the duplicated TetherFi hotspot start, stops the keep-alive hammering, and adds the instrumentation that can settle why the hotspot does not stay up while the car is parked.

**The proven bug.** Two uncoordinated dispatchers each launched TetherFi's tile Activity on every DashCast start — `HotspotKeeper`'s first heartbeat pass at ~T+0.8 s, and `BootReceiver`'s own **blind** `postDelayed(7 s)` dispatch, which never checked whether the hotspot was already on. Measured on-car at 13.9 s apart (`INC-20260721-184844`). `BootReceiver` no longer builds an Intent at all: it asks the keeper for one immediate probe-then-start pass, still gated on its own `PREF_HOTSPOT_AUTOSTART_BOOT` and still inside the `goAsync` accounting. A single dedupe rule, expressed on **dispatches** rather than on arms — the distinction an earlier attempt got wrong — is checked by both entry points: hotspot already UP at ACC-on now yields **zero** popups (the literal "check first" requirement), hotspot DOWN yields exactly **one**. The launch claim carries a timestamp with a 5-minute stuck-reset and the route loop re-checks it every iteration, so a wedged launch thread can neither silence the keep-alive for the life of the process nor return later and draw a second tile.

**Cadence.** The old policy (5 attempts, then a 5-minute blackout, forever) measured ~43 attempts/h overnight and was blacked out at the exact moment ACC came on. Replaced by a capped ladder (60/60/120/120/300 s) driven by **attempts**, so it engages even when every route fails: 15 attempts in hour 1, 12/h sustained, never permanently silent, with lower sustained shell traffic than the code it replaces.

**The hedge, and the honest part.** The tile is now launched via the uid-2000 shell (daemon, then local ADB, then the original in-app route), each tried at most once and called directly rather than through `ShellGateway`, which replays a failed command and would turn one attempt into two popups. This is a cheap bet, **not a fix**: the parked-car failure (~185 dispatches over 13.8 h, AP never up) remains un-root-caused, and since Android 10 logs `Background activity start blocked` unconditionally and no such line exists in any capture, the tile may well have been launching all along with TetherFi declining to raise the AP. The real deliverable is therefore the diagnostic: a +8 s confirmation re-probe, whose process snapshot rides on probes that already run, reports `tile DID run (TetherFi's process was created by this dispatch), TetherFi did not raise the AP` vs `tile did NOT run (launch suppressed)` vs an explicit `INCONCLUSIVE` when TetherFi's process predates the dispatch. Process ids are diffed as a **set**, so an unrelated sub-process cannot forge a confident verdict — the previous implementation asserted "the tile ran" from a non-empty `pidof` alone, which was wrong for every attempt after the first.

**Also.** The two hotspot prefs keep their independent meanings (an earlier attempt coupled them, making the page switch non-authoritative and letting one transient `PackageManager` miss erase "start at vehicle boot"). Testers without "Display over other apps" now see tile popups where the predecessor showed none — intended, since that permission only ever mattered to the in-app route and those users previously got neither a popup nor a hotspot. Two earlier designs were rejected outright by adversarial review (the first delayed the power-on start by ~60 s and left the keep-alive inert without a shell; the second reintroduced the double popup); this is the simplified third. No daemon protocol change; DL3/DL5, mirror, touch, CAN/HUD and OTA untouched. One string across thirteen locales; 164 unit tests green.

### 1.6.147-beta (versionCode 588)

Fixes three independent regressions reported together after an ACC off/on cycle (`INC-20260724-090820`, `INC-20260724-102136`, DiLink 3.0). Each root cause was adversarially re-verified against the raw captures before any code was written, and the three symptoms are proven independent — there is no ADB transport failure anywhere in the capture where the launches failed.

**Mirror touch did nothing.** Since `1.6.146` the MirrorDaemon deliberately survives a relaunch, and that is precisely what broke touch: because the daemon is *reused*, its Binder is already in `ServiceManager` when `MainActivity` starts, so it resolved **3.4 s before `ClusterService` existed**, the push path evaluated `if (mServiceBound) mClusterService else null` → `null` and dropped it. Nothing recovers afterwards — `onServiceConnected` never re-applies it, `onStart` only retries while the Binder is null, and no `ACTION_DAEMON_READY` follows a reused (never re-spawned) daemon. The forwarder stayed Binder-less all session and every touch fell back to the app-side path, which lacks `INJECT_EVENTS`: forty touches computed, correctly mapped, logged, and silently discarded. `ClusterService.onCreate` now **pulls** the registered Binder instead of waiting to be pushed one. The coordinate mapping was not at fault and is unchanged.

**An app would not launch on the cluster** until a different app was launched first — three attempts, each failing with the same `NullPointerException` in `ActivityStack.getBounds()`. DashCast manufactures the blocker itself: a launch creates a **fullscreen** stack, DashCast then docks it to split-screen-primary to apply the app's inset rect, and when that task dies WindowManager **orphans the docked stack** (an emptied fullscreen stack is destroyed instead — which is why an app with no configured inset never triggered it). The recovery routine looked like it worked but did not: `IActivityTaskManager.removeStack(int)` finishes the tasks *inside* a stack, so on a zero-task orphan it is a silent no-op that returns normally, and the code counted it as removed anyway — logging a successful removal **eleven consecutive times** while the same stack survived every pass. The removal is now verified against a re-read stack list, and escalates once to `removeStacksInWindowingModes` (which destroys the stack *object*), hard-gated on every split-screen stack system-wide being task-less so a live projection can never be dissolved. Recurrence of `INC-20260714-215700`, whose remedy never actually worked; the mechanism was documented correctly, only the efficacy of the removal call was wrong.

**A false "ADB not available" toast** fired while everything worked. `ClusterService.onCreate` opens three local-ADB connections within ~45 ms; adbd tears down the colliding transports and the resulting exception was classified "no listener", advising `adb tcpip 5555` for a port whose TCP probe had just passed and which served the MirrorDaemon 429 ms later. The handshake is now serialised by a fair bounded semaphore (30 s cap, then proceeds unserialised so it can never wedge), retried once silently before any verdict is published, and a handshake failure after a *passing* probe gets a distinct transient state that never advises enabling ADB-over-TCP and takes the short circuit-breaker recheck. Deliberately **not** gated on the proxy being connected: direct ADB is not bootstrap-only (screenshot cleanup every 30 s, and the MirrorDaemon providing mirror *and* touch injection is spawned over it), so that shortcut would have muted genuine outages.

Three previously silent failure modes now leave evidence: a null Binder no longer logs "connected", the direct injection path reports its result once, and a missing `MotionEvent.setDisplayId` is no longer swallowed. One string across thirteen locales; no daemon protocol change.

### 1.6.146-beta (versionCode 587)

Lifecycle hardening from `INC-20260718-220538`. The incident's tablet-wide slowdown was dominated by Waze, a third-party BYD polling app, SurfaceFlinger, and a camera error loop, but DashCast amplified one normal Back/relaunch cycle by killing a healthy MirrorDaemon. Mirror startup is now idempotent when a live ServiceManager Binder, exactly one daemon PID, and a PID-bound marker all prove that the daemon came from the current APK build. Old OTA daemons, pre-marker daemons, duplicate processes, dead binders, and ROMs without a trusted ServiceManager entry keep the conservative kill/relaunch behavior. The check is performed through the existing bounded dadb shell path and adds no blocking Binder transaction or wire-protocol renumbering.

The same incident exposed a separate stopped-but-bound service state: after **Restore original cluster**, `stopSelf()` removed the started state while MainActivity's binding kept `ClusterService` alive; later projection restarts did not restore that state, so pressing Back destroyed the active service on unbind. Re-activation now sends an explicit foreground-service action, republishes the notification synchronously in `onStartCommand`, restores projection ownership, and then uses the existing native platform guard. Initial startup, automatic Layout ownership, headless boot launch, DL3 single-OS suppression, normal DL3, DL4, and DL5 behavior remain on their existing paths. Seven focused daemon-identity tests cover current/stale builds, missing and stale markers, dead binders, Android `ps` parsing, and duplicate daemons.

### 1.6.145-beta (versionCode 586)

Fixes the experimental CarPlay diagnostic tool, which shipped in the published `1.6.144-beta` in a form that fought the head unit's own system-bar setting. Its "fullscreen" button wrote `policy_control="immersive.full=<package>"`; `policy_control` is a single global string that the OEM "hide bars" toggle also owns as `immersive.full=*`, so scoping the write to one package narrowed that wildcard and made the status and navigation bars reappear for every other window — while CarPlay itself still did not go fullscreen. The button now hides the bars for **all** apps via the same `immersive.full=*` wildcard the OEM uses, and leaves the setting untouched when it is already global-immersive (`isOemGlobalImmersive`), so it can no longer clobber the OEM state; the prior value is still saved before any write and Restore reverts it. A new read-only **Dump state** button (`settings`/`dumpsys` only, no `wm` verb, so it trips neither the display-0 guard nor the DL2 resize guard) reports display-0 geometry, the focused CarPlay window, bar visibility and the AutoKit layer's `activeBuffer`/`crop` — which reveals whether any remaining black bands are the adapter's fixed render size, something `policy_control` cannot change. Diagnostic-only; no daemon protocol change and no CAN or HUD rendering change; DL3/DL5 unaffected. `1.6.144-beta` was already published with the clobbering version because this fix was committed after that release was built.

### 1.6.144-beta (versionCode 585)

Restores HUD arrows in imperial locales and lands a corrected "is a route running?" gate. (`1.6.143-beta`/584 was built but never published; it is superseded by this release, which contains it in fixed form.)

**The product bug.** The distance parser was metric-only (`km|км|m|м`), so in a UK/US locale — where Google Maps and Waze post "in 500 ft" and "0.5 mi" — not one frame of an entire route could ever parse, and the windshield HUD arrow never worked there at all. Imperial units (`mi`/`ft`/`yd`, plus Italian `mt`) are now parsed and converted. Alternation is longest-first and remaining-time text such as "25 min" or "45 мин" still cannot match, so a duration is never misread as a distance; validated off-car across 17 cases.

**The gate.** On-car evidence from two 1.6.142 reports — the fields added in 1.6.140 confirmed both earlier gates work — showed the dominant cause of "no arrow" reports is that no route was ever started: the tester answered `HudNavApp: waze`, his firmware is arrow-capable and Waze is supported, so both gates correctly let him through, yet the rolling cluster screenshots show Waze idle on its "where to?" screen at 0 km/h for the whole capture, with zero navigation notifications and zero `NAV PARSE`. An adversarial pre-release review of the first attempt at this gate confirmed twelve of fifteen findings and showed it was net-harmful, so it was held back and rewritten rather than shipped. `MapNotificationListenerService` now tracks, per package, both when a supported nav app **posted** guidance and when a frame fully **parsed**. "Posted but never parsed" is a DashCast parser bug — the most valuable no-arrow report there is, and exactly what an imperial-locale driver would have produced — and is now sent straight through instead of being dismissed as "you never started a route". Missing notification access gets its own explanation rather than the same false no-route message. A generous thirty-minute recency window (the wizard is used *after* the drive) replaces the latch-forever flag, so one route no longer makes every later report claim a live route, and `onListenerConnected` replays already-posted notifications so a mid-route rebind resumes the HUD instead of looking idle. Every HUD report now carries `NavSeen:` with an age, distinguishing `yes` / `parse-fail` / `stale` / `no` / `notif-access-off`.

**Also.** HUD-only answers are cleared when leaving the HUD category (they leaked onto unrelated reports via Back), and the report records only DashCast's own notification grant as a boolean instead of uploading the driver's full list of notification-reading apps. The 1.6.140 `ActiveNav:` line is documented as a hint: it flags a merely *resident* OEM navigation, and Telenav is always resident on these European cars, so it fires even for a genuine Waze user — prefer `NavSeen`. One string across thirteen locales; no daemon protocol change and no CAN or HUD rendering change.

### 1.6.142-beta (versionCode 583)

HUD bug-report self-diagnosis, a nav-app gate, and three OEM-fidelity navigation-piloting gains, rolling up `1.6.140`–`1.6.142`. Based on `INC-20260718-114114`, most HUD "no arrow" reports are unsupported-navigation cases — the car runs the OEM Telenav navigation, which posts no notification, so DashCast's notification-driven HUD path has nothing to translate and the notification-triggered "unsupported navigation" log stays silent. A HUD report now probes the running-process list through the existing privileged shell and records `ActiveNav: <package> (unsupported — no notification path)`, so the report names the culprit; the HUD wizard also asks which navigation app the driver relies on and tells built-in / OEM users DashCast only mirrors Google Maps or Waze (five new strings across thirteen locales). A field-by-field comparison against the decompiled OEM `AmapService.sendNavigateInfoToCAN` (the factory instrument-cluster piloting path, sixteen registers to DashCast's eleven) drove three additive, DiLink-3-only, dedup-guarded gains that leave the video-confirmed icon/distance path untouched: the navigation-status register (`0x43E0003A`) is now re-asserted on every guidance frame to match the factory heartbeat; the arrival wall-clock ETA family (`0x43F09010`/`18`/`20`/`28`, "arrive at HH:MM") is parsed best-effort from the notification and sent; and a roundabout exit number ("3rd exit" / "sortie 3") is promoted to the OEM exit-numbered icon (CCW 25–34 / CW 35–44) instead of a generic circle. No daemon protocol change. Regex parsing validated off-car (17/17); on-car verification of the rendered fields pending. The known ceiling — second/lead maneuver, smooth per-second countdown, roundabout handedness, ETA calendar-day — is not in a Maps/Waze notification and is not attempted.

### 1.6.139-beta (versionCode 580)

Deterministic Layout task anchoring and confirmed ADB outage diagnostics after 1.6.138-beta. Based on `INC-20260718-095242`, the single short fission watchdog is replaced by independent package guardians that adopt recreated tasks, prioritize wrong-display tasks, correct late and repeated Waze rebounds, and remain active through a bounded startup horizon. Additive proxy protocol v21 cancels guardians before move, release, or full Layout teardown. Based on `INC-20260718-102309`, a stuck MirrorDaemon launch stream no longer produces a false restart-adbd warning: startup is single-flight and fully detached, a live registered Binder suppresses only that isolated timeout, and ordinary stream timeouts require a fresh independent ADB echo failure before becoming global `ADB_UNRESPONSIVE`. Seventeen focused tests cover timing, generations, multi-task selection, teardown, Binder numbering, timeout confirmation, and shell syntax.

See the [complete English 1.6.139-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.139-beta.md) for both incident timelines, guardian and transport architecture, protocol compatibility, D50F guarantees, validation evidence, APK identity, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.138-beta (versionCode 579)

Reliable automatic OTA installation and relaunch after 1.6.137-beta. The updater now reconnects the uid-2000 ProxyDaemon instead of requiring it to be alive at one exact instant, stages the downloaded APK under `/data/local/tmp` with exact-size verification before `pm install -r`, and preserves the interactive PackageInstaller path when privileged installation is unavailable. API 31+ sessions explicitly request no user action where Android permits it, while a durable relaunch marker shared by `STATUS_SUCCESS` and `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` reopens DashCast after either silent or user-confirmed replacement without creating duplicate tasks. Version comparison and generated shell syntax now have focused regression coverage.

See the [complete English 1.6.138-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.138-beta.md) for every change since 1.6.137-beta, install-path ownership, Android/DiLink feasibility limits, first-upgrade caveat, validation evidence, APK identity, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.137-beta (versionCode 578)

Timeout-safe local ADB and bounded D50F recovery after 1.6.136-beta. DashCast now uses dadb 2.0.0 with explicit connect/read/write deadlines, typed transport states, a forced lazy handshake, authorization-aware retry windows, and circuit-breaking that prevents a silent port-5555 peer from permanently occupying all ADB workers. A live ProxyDaemon Binder remains usable during a local-ADB outage, while actual legacy fallbacks fail fast. Bug Wizard and Bug Report now remain responsive without ADB, D50F cluster display selection prefers the real PRESENTATION display instead of hard-coding display 1, and SysInfo exposes the protocol-level diagnosis. All new user-facing diagnostics are translated in all 13 supported languages.

See the [complete English 1.6.137-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.137-beta.md) for every change since 1.6.136-beta, timeout and recovery policies, DiLink compatibility guarantees, validation evidence, known D50F limitation, APK identity, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.135-beta (versionCode 576)

Race-safe background optimization after 1.6.134-beta. Mirror startup no longer waits for its diagnostic SurfaceFlinger dump, while the M7 evidence remains available through a bounded asynchronous audit. Screenshot capture, pull, prune, and clear operations are now ordered on one worker; successful daemon pruning suppresses redundant shell work without removing the failed-capture fallback, disabling capture cannot leave a late JPEG behind, and report bundling cannot pull a partially written file. The visible Hotspot page collects service state and clients in one snapshot, avoids rebuilding unchanged rows, rejects callbacks from paused Activity generations, and prevents duplicate shell probes across pause/resume. Voice telemetry also removes repeated singleton and clock work while hidden.

See the [complete English 1.6.135-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.135-beta.md) for every change since 1.6.134-beta, the confirmed race analyses, fallback and compatibility guarantees, validation evidence, deferred high-risk candidates, APK identity, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.134-beta (versionCode 575)

Full-application runtime optimization after 1.6.133-beta. Startup storage hygiene now runs once per process, launcher shortcut discovery collapses its normal N Binder calls into one vendor-safe query, and cluster task lookup uses the existing typed daemon ATM verb before either dumpsys fallback. The Journal now applies generation- and eviction-safe incremental updates, wake-word PCM transfer and ONNX outputs avoid the largest lock-held loops and nested output allocations, high-frequency voice diagnostics are demand-gated, screenshot cleanup avoids a redundant shell round trip, and Cluster Resize reuses its fixed gesture-exclusion rectangles. The release also includes four focused policy/copy test suites and the complete 51k-line application audit, while deliberately deferring R8, structural layout rewrites, and behavior-sensitive polling changes.

See the [complete English 1.6.134-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.134-beta.md) for every optimization since 1.6.133-beta, fallback and compatibility guarantees, validation evidence, deferred candidates, APK identity, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.133-beta (versionCode 574)

Leaner daemon monitoring and a clean lint baseline after 1.6.132-beta. The always-on `ProxyKeeperService` now owns daemon health checks while active, allowing the redundant foreground `ProxyWatchdog` thread and wakeup stream to be released; foreground fallback monitoring returns automatically if the keeper stops. This release also fixes the inherited `SuspiciousIndentation` and `UseAppTint` correctness errors, removes three unnecessary baseline measurements, and eliminates Cluster Resize root overdraw through a color-preserving dedicated window theme. Lint now reports zero errors and zero warnings, with only 17 documented informational layout metrics remaining.

See the [complete English 1.6.133-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.133-beta.md) for the proxy ownership lifecycle, fallback guarantees, lint fixes, low-risk layout measurements, updated performance audit, validation evidence, compatibility notes, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.132-beta (versionCode 573)

Truthful D50F_LC physical-routing diagnostics after 1.6.131-beta. Based on `INC-20260717-221007`, this release corrects the false assumption that Znav on Android display 2/layerStack 2 or in a DashCast screenshot means the physical instrument panel changed. It preserves the native `IAutoContainer.sendInfo` integer through additive proxy protocol v20, classifies `ffffffff` as rejection `-1`, and adds a strictly D50F_LC-gated `D12` probe for the previously untested `1000/18 -> 6 s -> 16/35 -> observe -> restore` handshake. Production projection, launch, mirror, and input behavior remain unchanged on DiLink 3, DiLink 4, and normal DiLink 5; the candidate layerStack-1 `remote_dashboard` bridge remains intentionally deferred until the physical car confirms the switch.

See the [complete English 1.6.132-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.132-beta.md) for the incident evidence, native-result IPC, guarded handshake sequence, false-positive removal, clean-room physical-routing architecture, validation results, compatibility guarantees, known limitation, and stationary vehicle test procedure.

### 1.6.131-beta (versionCode 572)

Visible Layout activity state and switchable tactile mirrors after 1.6.130-beta. Based on `INC-20260717-153708`, Layout-launched applications now show the green active bar in both the app list and Favorites. Main can directly mirror and control a headlessly created Layout slot, select a running Layout app by tapping its tile, and cycle previous/next from an overlay that remains available in fullscreen. Video uses the slot's resolved layer stack, touch targets its display ID, multi-touch coordinates are mapped to the slot's real dimensions, and the selected task receives best-effort focus. Successful `launchAndForce` cascades also stop producing false non-OK warnings.

See the [complete English 1.6.131-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.131-beta.md) for the incident evidence, indicator fix, slot-selection behavior, tactile mirror architecture, lifecycle details, validation results, compatibility notes, and suggested vehicle checks.

Reliable automatic Layout ownership and DiLink 3 application-launch recovery after 1.6.129-beta. This release fixes `INC-20260717-122902` and `INC-20260717-175749`: Automatic favourite Layout now takes priority over a standalone app's Auto-launch at start setting, a sole usable saved Layout can repair a missing favourite, returning from Settings/Layout Manager re-evaluates startup immediately, and failed attempts can retry. On affected DiLink 3 ROMs, the known `ActivityStack.getBounds()` FREEFORM-stack crash now triggers a second stack cleanup before a plain launch. The misleading "not installed or incompatible with multi-display" message is replaced by an accurate launch-failure message in all 13 locales.

See the [complete English 1.6.130-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.130-beta.md) for the full incident analysis, startup precedence, Auto-Boot requirements, WindowManager recovery, validation results, compatibility notes, and suggested vehicle checks.

### 1.6.129-beta (versionCode 570)

Reliable Layout-mode projection teardown after 1.6.128-beta. This release fixes `INC-20260716-215316` and `INC-20260716-215226`: on the affected DiLink 3 ROM, the direct task-move API is absent even though the compatible stack-move API is available, while the previous slot release and asynchronous force-stop sequence raced native-cluster restoration. Layout shutdown now falls back through `moveStackToDisplay`, processes every package in strict move -> remove task -> verified force-stop -> slot release order, verifies process death with PID rechecks and `kill -9` escalation, and delays BYD/original-cluster restoration until all Layout applications have been handled.

See the [complete English 1.6.129-beta release notes](docs/releases/1.6.129-beta.md) for the full incident analysis, implementation details, validation results, compatibility notes, and suggested vehicle checks.

Resource, lifecycle, IPC, and concurrency hardening after 1.6.127-beta. | Version | versionCode | Summary |
|---------|-------------|---------|
| **1.2.72-beta** | 332 | **DL5 Fission resize battery refocused — 7 scattered probes collapsed into 3 focused shots, novel "resize the STACK not the task" avenue introduced.** Field reports v1.2.59 + v1.2.60 + v1.2.61 proved the per-verb exploration approach (F10/F11/F12/F12A/F12B/F12C/F12E) had exhausted the obvious shell surface without finding a working path: `set-task-windowing-mode` stripped, `task resize` silent no-op, `set-display-windowing-mode` stripped, `--activity-launch-bounds` unknown option. Rather than add a 13th nano-probe we collapse the whole F10..F12E block into 3 sharp probes that go straight at the remaining hole. **F10 — Topology.** Captures `taskId` (from F02/F05 confirmation), `stackId` containing that task (parsed from `dumpsys activity activities` via `Stack #N` or `stackId=N`), current `mBounds` + `mWindowingMode`, plus a compact inventory of resize-relevant verbs (`cmd activity stack`, `cmd activity task`, `am stack`, `wm`). One shell roundtrip, pure read-only. Row message: `taskId=N, stackId=M, verbs: stack-resize=Y/N, task-resize=Y/N, resizeable=Y/N`. **F11 — DL3 chain via shell.** Single atomic shell script that reproduces the DL3 production resize chain via every known shell variant. Step 1: `cmd activity task resizeable <taskId> 2` (unlock, was F12E). Step 2: tries 3 NEVER-BEFORE-TESTED stack-level verbs — `am stack resize <stackId>`, `cmd activity stack resize <stackId>`, `cmd window stack resize <stackId>` — because the DL3 production path actually calls `IActivityTaskManager.resizeStack(stackId, Rect)` not `resizeTask`. Step 3: retries `cmd activity task resize <taskId>` (in case step 1's resizeable=2 unlocked the silent no-op). Step 4: dumpsys diff. Each step's exit code captured; row message reports which verbs were accepted (`accepted verbs: am-stack-resize, cmd-window-stack-resize, …`). PASS = at least one verb mutated `mBounds`. **F12 — Verify final bounds.** Re-dumps after a 2 s settle and reports the winning verb. The DL3 production code path actually calls `IActivityTaskManager.resizeStack(stackId, Rect)` first — a different AIDL method on a different object. We have never tried the shell verb for `resizeStack` on DL5, and if any of the 3 variants in F11 step 2 is honoured, we have a real resize path. **Strictly additive on F01..F09 + F13..F16** — those rows (projection open, app launch on cluster display, user confirm, cleanup) are byte-identical to v1.2.60-beta. **SKIP outside DL5.** No production runtime change. **Sources.** `log/byd_report_20260528_081206.log`, `log/byd_report_20260528_172327.log`, plus the DL3 production resize path in `Phase4Verbs.moveAndResize` (already shipped and working on Seal EU). |

(4) `MainActivity` setup hides `btn_toggle_resize` + `panel_resize` (`View.GONE`) on a DL5 device whose probe returned `"no"`, so the user never sees a broken affordance; an info-level `AppLogger.i` line documents the gate ("Resize UI hidden: cluster task resize is not supported on this ROM"). **Reversibility.** A future BYD ROM update that restores `set-task-windowing-mode` is auto-detected on the next cold start once the user wipes the SharedPreferences key (or via a future Settings reset) — no code change required. All four hooks are guarded by `Platform.isDiLink5(ctx)` so DL2/DL3/DL4 paths are byte-identical to v1.2.58-beta. **Docs.** New `doc_api/DL5_CLUSTER_RESIZE_LIMITATION.md` (full F10/F11/F12 verbatim + AOSP-side rationale + three RE-able avenues for an actual DL5 resize : `sendInfo` cluster-side resize, an owned VirtualDisplay mirroring the Dilink5 Dashboard pattern, or a BYD-signed sideloaded helper). **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (318→319, 1.2.58-beta→1.2.59-beta), `platform/Platform.java` (+probe + sticky cache, ~110 LoC), `DashCastApp.java` (+`primeClusterResizeProbe` startup call), `ClusterService.java` (+import Platform, +`sResizeUnsupportedLogged` flag, +short-circuit at `resizeActiveTask` entry), `MainActivity.java` (+visibility gate before SeekBar listener wiring), `doc_api/DL5_CLUSTER_RESIZE_LIMITATION.md` (new). |

| **1.2.55-beta** | 314 | **Field-validated bug-fix pass after v1.2.54 device test (4 fixes, English).** Tester report: (1) launching Waze from the Apps screen required a double tap — process spawned on first tap but the app never appeared on the cluster, second tap fixed it; (2) the cluster mirror preview was still black at first launch despite the v1.2.52 fix — force-stop+relaunch or fullscreen+back fixed it; (3) the app's storage grew to 1.08 GB without ever opening the Sniffer panel, and the Sniffer's "Clean" action dropped it back to ~45 MB; (4) the "Hey Jarvis" wake-word switch did nothing. Each fix is strictly additive, gated, and idempotent — no DL3/DL5 regression. **Bug 1 — Waze first-tap fix.** Root cause: `am start` with `ActivityOptions.setLaunchDisplayId(targetDisplayId)` is honoured for most apps, but for `singleInstance` / `taskAffinity` apps that have NO running process at launch time (cold launch), AOSP routes the task to display 0 anyway. The user then sees the launcher (still on display 0) instead of the cluster, taps a second time, and the second `am start` re-targets the now-existing task correctly. Fix: in `MainActivity.onSendToDashboard`, right after the normal-launch path returns `launched=true`, schedule a 2.5 s `postDelayed` that calls a new `ClusterService.enforceTaskOnDisplay(pkg, targetDisplayId)`. The new method finds the running task via the existing `findRunningTaskId` (reuses AM `getRecentTasks` → daemon dumpsys → AdbLocal dumpsys ladder), and if the task exists, issues a direct `IActivityTaskManager.moveTaskToDisplay(taskId, displayId)` reflection call. If the task is not yet present (process still spawning), it silently no-ops (the user can re-tap; no fresh launch is forced, never duplicates the original `am start`). Skipped via the existing `PKG_FORCE_FRESH_LAUNCH` guard for TeleNav-style apps that explicitly need a fresh task. **Bug 2 — daemon-arrives-late mirror still black.** Root cause: cold start sequence can race so that `attemptStartMirrorWithCurrentHolder` runs before the MirrorDaemon Binder arrives. On DL3/DL5 the direct path silently "succeeds" (the SurfaceControl mirror token is built but no `ACCESS_SURFACE_FLINGER` ⇒ no frames). When the daemon Binder later arrives via `mDaemonReadyReceiver` or `tryGetDaemonBinderFromServiceManager`, the existing call to `attemptStartMirrorWithCurrentHolder` short-circuits at `if (isMirrorActive()) return;`, so the mirror stays black until the user goes fullscreen+back (which destroys the SurfaceTexture → `stopClusterMirror` → flag reset → restart). Fix: track which path established the active mirror in `ClusterMirrorManager` via a new boolean `mMirrorViaDaemon` (set true in `startMirrorViaDaemon` daemon-success branch, set false in the direct-path success branch, reset in `stopPreview` and `stopMirrorViaDaemon`) and expose `isMirrorViaDaemon()`. In MainActivity, both daemon-arrival sites now check `mm.isMirrorActive() && !mm.isMirrorViaDaemon()`; if true they call `stopClusterMirror()` first, so the subsequent `attemptStartMirrorWithCurrentHolder` re-establishes the mirror via the daemon path. Daemon-first cold starts (the common case) and direct-path-only environments (in-app SurfaceFlinger access) are unaffected. **Bug 3 — 1.08 GB storage growth without sniffer ever opened.** Root cause #1 — file accumulation: every save-log / save-report / sniffer run writes a fresh timestamped file (`byd_log_*.log`, `byd_report_*.log`, `BYD_RE_Sniffer_*.txt`) into `getExternalFilesDir(null)` and nothing rotates them, so disk usage grows monotonically across testing sessions. Root cause #2 — orphan sniffer processes: `DiagActivity.startSniffer` uses `setsid` to detach background `logcat` processes from the app process group so they survive force-stop. The cleanup tag (`/data/local/tmp/.re_sniffer_run`) and PID list (`/data/local/tmp/.re_sniffer_pids`) are only consulted when the user re-enters the Sniffer tab (`restoreSnifferState`), so a user who simply restarts the app without visiting that tab keeps writing to the latest `BYD_RE_Sniffer_*.txt` indefinitely. Fix: new `AppLogger.pruneOldFiles(Context, int keepPerPrefix)` groups files by prefix in both `extFilesDir` and `filesDir`, sorts by `lastModified` desc, deletes everything past `keepPerPrefix` (called with `3` from `MainActivity.onCreate`). New orphan-sniffer cleanup invoked once at `MainActivity.onCreate` via `ShellGateway.execShell`: if `/data/local/tmp/.re_sniffer_run` exists, kill every PID listed in `/data/local/tmp/.re_sniffer_pids`, then `pkill -f BYD_RE_Sniffer_` as a belt-and-suspenders sweep, then remove both tag files. The user-driven "Clean" path in the Sniffer tab is untouched. **Bug 4 — wake-word switch is a no-op.** Root cause: `DiagActivity.onWakeWordToggle(true)` creates `WakeWordEngine` and registers it as `VoiceService.setSampleConsumer(engine)`, but the engine only receives audio frames when `VoiceService` is actually capturing — and the service is only started by the separate "Démarrer l'écoute" button. A user enabling the switch without first hitting "Démarrer" sees nothing happen. Fix: `onWakeWordToggle` now auto-starts `VoiceService` if `!VoiceService.isRunning()`, requesting `RECORD_AUDIO` first if needed (and visibly snapping the switch back OFF in that case so the user knows to re-toggle after the grant). **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (313→314, 1.2.54-beta→1.2.55-beta), `ClusterMirrorManager.java` (+`mMirrorViaDaemon` field + `isMirrorViaDaemon()` + 4 set/reset sites, ~12 LoC), `ClusterService.java` (+`enforceTaskOnDisplay` method, ~30 LoC), `MainActivity.java` (onCreate: prune + orphan-sniffer kill; onSendToDashboard normal-launch success: deferred enforce; daemon-arrival sites: stop+restart if direct-path stale; ~70 LoC total), `AppLogger.java` (+`pruneOldFiles`, ~45 LoC), `DiagActivity.java` (+ permission + auto-start in `onWakeWordToggle`, ~17 LoC). No new strings, no new layouts, no new permissions. **Zero behavioural regression.** All four fixes are gated and idempotent; existing successful flows on DL3/DL5 take the same code paths as v1.2.54. |

| **1.2.49-beta** | 307 | **DL5 Fission task-id extraction hardening.** Third field test (`log/byd_report_20260525_152233.log`, 11 PASS / 1 WARN / 0 FAIL / 4 SKIPPED) was the first run where the new interactive multi-display probe worked end-to-end: F01 opened the cluster projection, F04 launched Yandex on display 2 (hidden, the testeur correctly answered NO), F06 launched it on display 3, F07's popup got a clean **YES** and the runner recorded display 3 as the cluster. Only one bug remained: the v1.2.48 awk-scoped `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay` returned empty on this ROM (the actual `dumpsys activity activities` layout does not match the `(mPreferredTopFocusableRootTask\|mLastFocusedRootTask)=Task{…A=…yandexmaps}` pattern that v1.2.47/48 inherited from the v1.2.46 ROM dumps), so `yandexTaskIdOnCluster = -1` → F10 WARN « no taskId extracted for confirmed display 3 » → F11 SKIPPED « no taskId (F10) » → F12 SKIPPED « no taskId from F10 ». The actual resize therefore never ran. **Root-cause analysis.** Two fragile assumptions: (a) scoping the task to a specific displayId via dump structure is ROM-dependent and we have no way to know which DiLink5 firmwares ship which layout without burning a field test for each; (b) the task may not be fully registered in the dump at the exact moment the user taps YES, especially if Yandex has been bouncing between displays in the previous candidate steps. **Fix.** Drop the per-display scoping entirely — since the runner force-stops Yandex before every candidate launch (F04/F06/F08 all start with `am force-stop`), exactly ONE Yandex task exists at the moment of YES. So a global match for `Task{…ru.yandex.yandexmaps…}` is sufficient and ROM-agnostic. The new extractor `extractYandexTaskId(Context, StringBuilder debugOut)` does: (1) a primary regex `Task\{[^}]*ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps[^}]*\}` then `#(\d+)` on the matched substring, (2) a fallback regex `taskId=(\d+)[^\n]*ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps` for ROMs that format tasks differently, (3) up to **4 attempts** with 0 / 250 / 500 / 1000 ms backoff so a late-registered task is still caught (covers the case where the Yandex SplashScreen → main activity transition hasn't published the task at the exact YES tap). The dumpsys read is filtered through `grep -nE 'Task\{\|taskId=\|yandexmaps' \| grep -i yandex \| head -20` so the captured debug stays compact (≤ 20 lines per attempt). On success the extractor returns the integer taskId; on failure it returns -1 and the full per-attempt debug capture (raw filtered dumpsys lines) is appended to `r.detail` of F07 (and re-captured in F10 if the prompt-time extraction had failed and F10 retries). This means **any future taskId extraction regression is debuggable from the field report alone** — no adb access required, no need to re-instrument the build. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (306→307, 1.2.48-beta→1.2.49-beta + per-version comment block), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (one method replaced — `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay(Context,int)` → `extractYandexTaskId(Context, StringBuilder)`; two call sites updated — `fissionPromptDisplay` and `fissionSetFreeformOnCluster` now both pass a StringBuilder and emit the debug capture into their respective `r.detail`; ~80 LoC net change, no API change outside the file). No new permission, no manifest change, no new string resource, no new layout, no new dependency, no DiagActivity change, no R-tier change. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.49 over v1.1.48, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm. Same exact UX as v1.2.48 (same projection activation, same per-display launch + Oui/Non popup, same NO bascule). Difference: when the testeur answers **Oui** on the cluster display, F07/F09 now reliably picks up the Yandex taskId (with up to 1.75 s of retry budget if needed), and **F10/F11/F12 actually run** — F10 forces the task into freeform via `cmd activity set-task-windowing-mode <tid> 5`, F11 resizes it to 100×80 → 1820×640, F12 verifies the new bounds. The exported report contains a new `--- task lookup debug ---` block under F07 with the raw dumpsys lines the extractor used; if extraction ever fails on a future ROM, that block plus the visible per-attempt output is sufficient to design the next fix without a roundtrip. F13/F14/F15/F16 cleanup unchanged. **Safety.** Strictly scoped to one method + its two call sites in the F-tier extraction path. Non-DL5 platforms see no behavioural difference (single SKIPPED row preserved). R-tier listener untouched. Popup logic, i18n keys, version-independent infra all untouched. |
| **1.2.48-beta** | 306 | **DL5 Fission runner — interactive multi-display probe + projection auto-activation.** Second field test (`log/byd_report_20260525_144058.log`, 9 PASS / 2 WARN / 1 FAIL) showed two distinct shortcomings of the v1.2.47 runner: (a) the awk parser matched the FIRST `A=…yandexmaps` occurrence in `dumpsys activity activities`, which on this ROM sits in the flat task list BEFORE any `displayId=` line — so `DISP=` came out empty and F06 was a false-negative FAIL while F10 was a false-negative WARN (Yandex actually had landed on display 3 in F05, then moved back to display 0 in F09 correctly). (b) Tasks launched on fission displays always end up `mode=fullscreen` even with `--windowingMode 5` because the display itself declares `mDisplayWindowingMode=fullscreen`, so the later `cmd activity task resize` is silently a no-op. Rather than keep chasing dumpsys heuristics to guess which fission display is the cluster, v1.2.48 rewrites the F-tier as an **interactive** probe that **asks the user**, **on each candidate display in turn**, whether Yandex is visible on the cluster screen — per the testeur's explicit request: « j'aimerais bien que le test fission active la projection Dilink5 et tente de lancer l'application sur le display 2 puis 3 puis 4. À chaque fois je voudrais : l'application se lance sur le display X puis pop up à l'utilisateur dans sa langue qui demande si l'application est bien sur le cluster. Si il répond oui, on passe aux test de resize etc de l'application. Si il répond non, on bascule l'app sur le display 0, on la tue et on la relance sur le display 3 pour refaire le même process et ainsi de suite ». **New F-tier flow (16 steps, ~30–90 s typical depending on user answers).** **F01** opens the BYD cluster projection itself (`service call auto_container 2 i32 1000 i32 16 s16 ""`, same call documented in `MirrorTestRunner.runM10`) so fission displays become routable; sleeps 2 s to let the compositor wire them. Sets `FissionState.projectionOpenedByUs = true` so F16 knows whether to tear down. **F02** unchanged from v1.2.47 — resolves Yandex Maps launcher activity via `getLaunchIntentForPackage`. **F03** unchanged — `am compat enable 174042936 ru.yandex.yandexmaps`. **F04 / F06 / F08** force-stop Yandex (so the new compat takes effect) then `am start --display N --windowingMode 5 -n <component>` for N in {2, 3, 4} respectively. Stdout is parsed for « unable to resolve » / « does not exist » / « invalid display » / « no display » → FAIL / WARN with display-specific message; success records `lastLaunchedDisplay` and sleeps 2.5 s so the user has time to look at the cluster. Each step SKIPs cleanly if a previous candidate was already confirmed by the user. **F05 / F07 / F09** are the new interactive prompts. The runner posts `Listener.onPromptYesNo(title, message, callback)` on the UI thread (new default method on `Dl5ClusterReconRunner.Listener` — non-DL5 listeners and the R-tier path keep working without changes, default = NO) and blocks on a `CountDownLatch` with a 180-s safety timeout. `DiagActivity` overrides `onPromptYesNo` to build a non-cancellable `AlertDialog` with two buttons (`diag_dl5_fission_prompt_yes` / `_no`) that invoke the callback. **YES branch** → records `confirmedClusterDisplay = displayId`, extracts the Yandex taskId via a properly-scoped awk pass (`extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay`: walks the dump, tracks the current `displayId=N` block, and only matches `(mPreferredTopFocusableRootTask\|mLastFocusedRootTask)=Task{…A=…yandexmaps}` INSIDE that block — the very awk fix the v1.2.47 false-negative diagnostic was missing), stores it in `yandexTaskIdOnCluster` for F10/F11/F12, marks PASS with « user confirmed cluster on display N (taskId=…) ». **NO branch** → fires `am start --display 0 -n <component>` to reparent Yandex back to the head unit, sleeps 1.2 s, fires `am force-stop` so the next candidate launches from a clean slate, marks PASS with « user said NO — display N is not the cluster ». **TIMEOUT branch** → WARN with « user prompt timed out (180 s) on display N ». Every step logs `prompt title` / `prompt msg` / `user answer` in `r.detail` so the field report captures exactly what the user saw and what they answered (in their locale). **F10** is new: `cmd activity set-task-windowing-mode <taskId> 5` on the confirmed cluster display, the workaround for the display-level fullscreen lock that made v1.2.47's `task resize` silently no-op. SKIPs cleanly if no display was confirmed. **F11** unchanged shape (`cmd activity task resize <taskId> 100 80 1820 640`) but now runs against the user-confirmed display + freeform-locked task. **F12** verifies the new bounds (same `-A 25` task-block scope as v1.2.47). **F13** moves Yandex back to display 0 via the resolved launcher. **F14 / F15 / F16** cleanup: force-stop Yandex, reset the FORCE_RESIZE_APP compat override, then close the projection (`sendInfo 18` + `sendInfo 0`) **only if F01 opened it** — never tears down a projection the user had already started manually. **i18n.** 4 new keys × 12 locales = 48 entries: `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_title`, `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_msg_fmt` (formatted with `%1$d` for the display id, with the standard `\n\n` UX separator between the question and the per-display context), `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_yes`, `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_no`. Hand-curated translations per locale; Uzbek apostrophes properly escaped as `\'`; zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (305→306, 1.2.47-beta→1.2.48-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (Listener gains `default onPromptYesNo(...)`; FissionState gets 4 new fields — `projectionOpenedByUs`, `confirmedClusterDisplay`, `yandexTaskIdOnCluster`, `lastLaunchedDisplay`; new `FISSION_CANDIDATE_DISPLAYS = {2,3,4}` constant; fissionCatalog rewritten with 16 entries; runFissionOne dispatcher rewritten; new step methods `fissionActivateProjection`, `fissionAttemptDisplay(int)`, `fissionPromptDisplay(int, Listener)`, `fissionSetFreeformOnCluster`, `fissionResizeOnCluster`, `fissionVerifyBoundsOnCluster`, `fissionMoveBackToMain`, `fissionCloseProjection`, `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay(int)`; old `fissionDetectDisplay` / `fissionLaunch` / `fissionVerifyOnFission` / `fissionResizeTask` / `fissionVerifyBounds` / `fissionMoveToMainDisplay` / `fissionVerifyOnMainDisplay` / `extractTaskMode` / `isFissionName` removed; ~530 LoC net), `DiagActivity.java` (+`onPromptYesNo` override on the fission `Listener` anonymous class, ~17 LoC), `res/values*/strings.xml` × 12 (+4 keys each via a single `multi_replace_string_in_file` batch). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new layout, no new drawable. **Zero behavioural regression** on any platform — the change is strictly scoped to the F-tier diagnostic battery. Non-DL5 platforms still see the same single SKIPPED row. The R-tier (R01..R30) catalog and runner are untouched. The R-tier Listener uses the new default `onPromptYesNo` (= NO) and never triggers a prompt because no F-step is in the R-catalog. The `Test Fission` button stays gated to `View.VISIBLE` only on `Platform.get().isAutoDetectedDiLink5()`, with the same confirm dialog as v1.2.41. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.48 over v1.2.47, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm: F01 opens the cluster projection (the testeur should see the BYD cluster wake up to a black/system surface within ~2 s), F02 resolves the Yandex launcher, F03 enables the compat override, then for each candidate display in {2, 3, 4} F04/F06/F08 launches Yandex there and F05/F07/F09 immediately popups « Yandex Maps est-il visible sur l'écran cluster ? (test sur le display N) » with Oui / Non buttons in the device locale. Saying **Non** routes Yandex back to display 0, kills it, and the next pair Fxx/Fxx+1 starts on the next candidate display. Saying **Oui** records that display as the cluster, skips the remaining candidates (they show SKIPPED with the message « display X already confirmed as cluster »), then F10 sets the task to freeform, F11 resizes it, F12 verifies the new bounds, F13 moves it back to display 0, F14 force-stops it, F15 resets the compat override, F16 closes the projection. Saying **Oui** on no display (or letting all three prompts time out) leaves F10–F12 as SKIPPED with « no cluster display confirmed », while cleanup F13–F16 still runs. The exported report via **Copy report** / **Send via Telegram** keeps the same `renderFissionReport` layout but now contains the user's per-display answers + the confirmed cluster display id + the on-display taskId, so the next diagnostic iteration knows exactly which display id to target. |
| **1.2.47-beta** | 305 | **DL5 Fission runner — fixes after first field run.** Field test on a real DL5 (run `log/byd_report_20260525_142241.log`) scored « 10 PASS / 2 WARN / 0 FAIL » but two of those PASS were false positives that hid a real launch failure : **F05** ran `am start -a MAIN -c LAUNCHER -p ru.yandex.yandexmaps` → `am` printed « Error: Activity not started, unable to resolve Intent » yet still exited 0, so the runner marked PASS. **F09** same intent shape → same false PASS. **F10** verified « `displayId=0` appears anywhere in the dump » which is always true on any device (display 0 is the main head-unit screen). Plus **F06/F08** grep filters were too broad : they matched bounds and windowing modes of unrelated parent/split tasks, never scoped to the Yandex task block. **Fixes (runner-only, zero UI change, zero behavioural change outside the F-tier diagnostic battery).** **F02** now also resolves the actual launcher `ComponentName` via `PackageManager.getLaunchIntentForPackage("ru.yandex.yandexmaps")` (returns `ru.yandex.yandexmaps/.SplashScreen` on the field ROM). The resolved component is stored in `FissionState.targetActivity`. If no launcher activity exists, F03..F10 SKIP cleanly with a precise message. **F05** rewritten as `am start --display N --windowingMode 5 -n <pkg/.Activity>` using the resolved component — bypasses the over-restrictive action/category filter that failed to match Yandex's launcher on this ROM. Stdout is now scanned for « unable to resolve » → mapped to FAIL (and `abortFromHere`) regardless of `am` exit code, so a silent intent-resolution failure can no longer hide behind exit 0. **F09** identical fix : `am start --display 0 -n <pkg/.Activity>` ; « unable to resolve » → WARN (cleanup-adjacent step, doesn't abort cleanup chain). **F06** rewritten with a two-pass dumpsys read : (1) the existing human-readable grep stays as the report detail block, (2) a new awk pass walks `dumpsys activity activities`, tracks the most recent `displayId=N`, and on the first `A=<uid>:ru.yandex.yandexmaps` line prints both `DISP=<displayId>` and `TASK=<full task line>`. The runner then parses `DISP=` as the authoritative per-task display id (no more false positives from substring sweeps) and extracts the windowing mode from the task line's own `mode=...` token via the new `extractTaskMode(taskLine)` helper. **F08** scopes `mBounds=Rect` extraction to `grep -A 25 -E '#<taskId>[^0-9]'` so it can only match lines that belong to the Yandex task block (and the regex `[^0-9]` makes #1499 not match #149). The check now requires BOTH the `(100, 80` origin AND the `1820` right edge on the same line, so a stray `100` from an unrelated config can no longer trigger a false PASS. The « bounds not confirmed » message is also clarified to point out the task is likely not visible / not yet relayouted (resize only takes effect on the next layout pass). **F10** same awk trick as F06 : finds the displayId that immediately precedes the Yandex task line in the dump hierarchy ; PASS iff `parsedDisplayId == 0`, WARN with the actual display id if the move did not take effect (e.g. « task still on display 3 (move not effective) »). Catalog descriptions for F02 / F05 / F09 rewritten to reflect the new logic + the field-test root cause. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (304→305, 1.2.46-beta→1.2.47-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (FissionState +`targetActivity`, F02/F05/F09 implementations rewritten, F06/F10 awk-scoped extraction + helper `extractTaskMode`, F08 task-block scoped grep, ~120 LoC net). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new string, no layout change. **Zero behavioural regression.** R-tier recon catalogs (R01..R30) and every other panel (Beta, DL2, DL4, Mirror, Cluster DL5 recon-v2, Voice, Export APK) are untouched — the change is strictly scoped to the F-tier diagnostic battery's 12 test implementations + its shared mutable state. Non-DL5 platforms still see the same single SKIPPED row. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.47 over v1.2.46, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm : F01 detects the fission display, F02 resolves the Yandex launcher activity (now visible in the F02 row message, e.g. « ru.yandex.yandexmaps installed, launcher=ru.yandex.yandexmaps/.SplashScreen »), F03 enables the FORCE_RESIZE_APP compat override, F04 force-stops Yandex, F05 launches Yandex on the fission display via the resolved component — if the launch fails because the intent can't be resolved, F05 is now FAIL (not PASS) and the rest of F06..F08 are SKIPPED. F06 reports the actual per-task displayId + windowing mode taken from the Yandex task line itself. F07 resizes the task. F08 reports whether the new bounds actually landed in the Yandex task block (not in some unrelated split-screen task). F09 moves the task back to display 0 via the resolved component. F10 confirms the move based on the displayId that immediately precedes the Yandex task in the dump (so if the task is still on display 3, the row says exactly that). F11+F12 cleanup are unchanged. The report rendered by **Copy report** / **Send via Telegram** keeps the same `renderFissionReport` layout but the per-step verdicts are now trustworthy. |
| **1.2.46-beta** | 304 | **Diagnostic — cold-start perf : lazy test-row preparation.** Field report : the Diagnostic page felt slow to open. **Root cause.** `DiagActivity.onCreate()` was eagerly inflating the test-row catalogs of all 6 diag panels — Beta Engine (28 rows) + DiLink 5 (31) + DL5 Cluster recon (42) + DiLink 2 (40) + DiLink 4 (40) + Mirror (10) = **191 row inflations × ~6 `findViewById` per row** on the UI thread, even though only one panel is ever visible at a time. On DL3/DL5 that's ~400–800 ms of bound UI-thread work before the first frame can be drawn. The 5 non-active panels' inflations are pure waste — the typical user opens Diag, looks at one tab, and goes back. **Fix.** Each `prepare<X>TestRows()` is now wrapped behind a lightweight `prepare<X>TestRowsIfNeeded()` guard (one `boolean <x>RowsPrepared` flag per panel) and the call moves out of `onCreate` into `showPanelForTab(position)` so the catalog is only inflated the first time that panel actually becomes visible. Once prepared, the guard is a no-op so subsequent tab switches stay free. The default tab (selected at the end of `onCreate` via `tabs.selectTab` → `showPanelForTab(defaultTab)`) still gets its rows ready before the first frame is drawn — so the user sees exactly the same content as before, just much faster. Pattern is identical to the existing `refreshExportApkListIfNeeded()` and `onVoicePanelEntered()` (which were already lazy since v1.2.42/v1.2.43). **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (303→304, 1.2.45-beta→1.2.46-beta), `DiagActivity.java` (+6 `boolean` fields, +6 `prepare<X>TestRowsIfNeeded` wrappers, removed 6 eager calls from `onCreate`, added 6 lazy calls in `showPanelForTab`). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new string, no new layout. **Zero behavioural regression.** Same rows, same bindings, same Run-All / Copy / Telegram pipelines — only the inflation moment changes (`onCreate` → first `showPanelForTab` for that tab). The default tab logic (`isAutoDetectedDiLink2 → TAB_DILINK2`, then DL4, then DL5, then Beta as fallback) is unchanged ; whichever tab opens first, its catalog is prepared synchronously inside `showPanelForTab` before the panel becomes visible. **Behavioural contract.** Any platform — install v1.2.46 over v1.2.45, open Diagnostic : the page opens noticeably faster (one catalog inflated instead of six). Switching to another tab inflates that catalog the first time and is instant on every subsequent switch. Every existing test, button, report and Telegram action keeps the exact same behaviour as v1.2.45. |
| **1.2.45-beta** | 303 | **Main — new « Mode compact (liste apps étroite) » Settings toggle : freed left column gives the cluster preview pane significantly more room.** Field request: a user reported wanting to shrink the apps column on the home screen to ~2 icons wide so the cluster preview on the right pane could grow (both in width and height) and use the screen real estate more efficiently. The user also explicitly asked for it to be a Settings switch so each driver can pick the layout they prefer rather than forcing a single behaviour. **New Settings pref.** `SettingsActivity.PREF_COMPACT_APPS_PANEL = "compact_apps_panel"` (default `false` → historical 5-column layout preserved, zero regression for users who don't enable it). Toggle row injected in `activity_settings.xml` right under the existing « Filtres par catégorie » row in the « Affichage » card, with the standard `ic_aspect_ratio` icon, headline `settings_compact_apps_panel` and support text `settings_compact_apps_panel_support`. Wired in `SettingsActivity` with the same pattern as every other boolean toggle (bindView → loadPreferences → setOnCheckedChangeListener → `getSharedPreferences(PREFS_NAME).edit().putBoolean(...).apply()` + `AppLogger.i`). **Layout switch in MainActivity.** New helper `applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` that reads the pref and applies three idempotent changes : (a) the left apps column's `layout_weight` flips between `1.4` (normal) and `0.6` (compact), achieved by mutating `llAppListSection.getLayoutParams()` on the existing `LinearLayout.LayoutParams` instance — no view re-inflation, no allocation per call ; (b) the `rv_apps` `GridLayoutManager` spanCount flips between `5` (normal) and `2` (compact), but only when currently in grid mode (list mode has no spanCount, so it's left untouched and still works fine in the narrower column) — guarded by `getSpanCount()` comparison so the LayoutManager is only re-created when actually needed ; (c) the filter chips row (`ll_category_filters`) is force-hidden in compact mode because the chip row no longer fits in a 2-icon column ; toggling compact OFF restores chips visibility per their own `PREF_SHOW_CATEGORY_FILTERS` pref so both prefs remain independent. **Live apply.** `applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` is called from `MainActivity.onResume()` (right after the existing `refreshNavHotspot()`), so the new layout kicks in as soon as the user comes back from Settings — no activity restart, no rebind dance, no flicker on cold start (`onResume` runs synchronously after `onCreate` before the first frame is drawn, so the initial column weight + spanCount are correct from the very first paint). **toggleViewMode() updated.** The list↔grid button in the search bar already re-creates the `GridLayoutManager` on every toggle ; the new `nv` branch now reads the compact pref to pick the right spanCount (`compact ? 2 : 5`), so toggling list→grid while in compact mode keeps the 2-column layout. **i18n.** 2 new keys × 12 locales = 24 entries. New keys : `settings_compact_apps_panel` (toggle headline), `settings_compact_apps_panel_support` (sublabel explaining « Libère de l'espace pour le preview cluster — 2 colonnes au lieu de 5 »). French (default) shipped manually with the proper UX phrasing ; 11 other locales (`en`, `de`, `es`, `it`, `tr`, `ru`, `uk`, `be`, `kk`, `uz`, `ar`) injected via the idempotent script `/tmp/i18n_v1_2_45.py` with hand-curated translations per locale (apostrophes properly escaped as `\'`, technical identifiers kept untouched). Zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (302→303, 1.2.44-beta→1.2.45-beta), `SettingsActivity.java` (+`PREF_COMPACT_APPS_PANEL` constant, +`cbCompactAppsPanel` field, +bindView/loadPreferences/listener wiring, ~25 LoC), `activity_settings.xml` (+1 toggle row in Display card), `MainActivity.java` (+`applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` method ~50 LoC, +1 call in `onResume`, `toggleViewMode()` updated to honour the spanCount), `res/values/strings.xml` + 11 other locales (+2 keys each). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new drawable (reuses existing `ic_aspect_ratio`). **Zero behavioural regression.** The pref defaults to `false`, so every user who upgrades to v1.2.45 without touching the new Settings toggle sees the exact same layout as v1.2.44. Users who toggle it ON get the compact layout instantly on return from Settings ; toggling it OFF restores the 1.4 weight + 5-col grid + the chips visibility per their own pref. **Behavioural contract.** Any platform — install v1.2.45 over v1.2.44, open Settings → Affichage → enable « Mode compact (liste apps étroite) » : the apps column narrows to a 2-icon grid, the cluster preview pane on the right expands to fill the freed horizontal space, and any visible chip row is hidden so the column stays clean. Disable the toggle → layout reverts to the historical 5-col grid + 1.4 weight + chip visibility per its own pref, immediately on return to Main. List↔grid view toggle (search bar button) keeps honouring compact mode through subsequent flips. |
| **1.2.44-beta** | 302 | **Hotspot — UX consistency pass : navrail unified across all secondary screens, uptime chrono now real-time, connected-clients enumeration fixed.** Four bugs reported from the field, all fixed in this release. **Bug 1 — navrail inconsistent between secondary screens.** Until now the « Hotspot » entry only existed on the Main activity navrail ; entering Diag / Sysinfo / Log / Settings made it visually disappear, breaking the user's mental model of « the navrail is always the same set of icons ». Fix : new helper `NavRailHotspot.apply(activity, viewId, finishOnNav)` in `com.byd.dashcast.NavRailHotspot` that replicates the existing runtime gating logic (DL3 + `use_own_sim` pref defaulting to true since v1.2.38) and wires the click handler. Each of the 4 secondary layouts (`activity_diag.xml`, `activity_log.xml`, `activity_sysinfo.xml`, `activity_settings.xml`) receives a new `LinearLayout` wrapper (id `nav_hotspot_diag` / `_log` / `_sys` / `_set`, `visibility="gone"` by default — the helper flips it to VISIBLE only when the gating passes), pinned just below the bottom weight spacer so it visually mirrors the position used on Main. The 4 activities each call `NavRailHotspot.apply(this, R.id.nav_hotspot_<suffix>, true)` from their existing `wire<Screen>NavRail()` method — single line of glue per screen. **Bug 2 — navrail missing entirely from Hotspot activity.** Entering Hotspot dropped the user into a screen with only a back button in the top bar, no navrail at all, forcing a finish-and-relaunch to reach any other screen. Fix : `activity_hotspot.xml` root changed from a single `NestedScrollView` to a horizontal `LinearLayout` containing : (a) a full navrail on the left (96 dp, Apps/Settings/Diag/Sysinfo/Log entries clickable + Hotspot entry marked active with `bg_nav_pill_active` and primary tint, matching the highlight style used on Log/Settings), (b) the original 2-col content wrapped in a `NestedScrollView` with weight=1. The original top bar (back button + title) is preserved unchanged inside the scroll view. `HotspotActivity` gets a new `wireHotspotNavRail()` method called from `onCreate` that wires the 5 other navrail items as `startActivity + finish` (same pattern used by Diag / Log / Sysinfo / Settings). The Hotspot entry itself is purely decorative (no click — we're already on Hotspot). **Bug 3 — uptime counter not refreshed in real time.** The session uptime chrono was only updated when the `statsTick` runnable fired, which happens every `STATS_PERIOD_MS` = 5 s (the ADB-backed presence probe period). The counter visibly jumped 5 seconds at a time, making it look frozen between updates.
